Reflection: Annual Budget Year 1

1. IMPORTANT EVENTS:

During the meeting, I consistently advocated for Option A, which proposes allocating 50% of total funds evenly across the four quarters. My position was grounded in the belief that a moderate, cautious approach best suits our first year, given the lack of historical data and the need to build a foundation of trust and collaboration within the team. I emphasized that Option A balances participation in market opportunities with prudent risk management, fostering a culture of psychological safety and disciplined learning.

When I presented my perspective, CEO Kelsie Schwartz acknowledged and appreciated the cultural emphasis I brought to the discussion, recognizing the importance of fostering collaboration and reflection alongside financial prudence. Although Kelsie initially favored Option C, which advocates full capital engagement, she showed openness to the arguments supporting Option A's caution and adaptability.

The voting outcomes reflected a clear divide. The CEO's original proposal for Option C was rejected with only 2 out of 6 votes in favor, including her own and Celine Nash’s. The majority, myself included, voted against it, preferring the more cautious Option A. Subsequently, the CEO proposed Option A as an alternative, which was unanimously approved by all shareholders. Thus, my preferred option was ultimately adopted as the final company decision.

2. CEO & COMPANY EVALUATION:

CEO Kelsie Schwartz demonstrated strong leadership in facilitating open, respectful dialogue and encouraging shareholders to articulate both financial and cultural considerations. She did well in summarizing key viewpoints and highlighting the importance of balancing ambition with flexibility, as seen when she incorporated the concerns about psychological safety and adaptability raised by myself and others.

However, the CEO could improve by more actively integrating dissenting perspectives earlier in the process to build stronger consensus before formal proposals. While she acknowledged alternative views during discussions, her initial firm preference for Option C may have contributed to the initial proposal’s rejection. A more collaborative approach from the outset might have streamlined decision-making.

The company’s decision-making process worked effectively in fostering thorough discussion, allowing divergent views to be expressed and considered. The structured rounds and CEO summaries helped clarify the options and their implications. The willingness to revisit the proposal after the vote demonstrated adaptability and respect for shareholder input.

Overall, I believe the final decision to adopt Option A was good for the company. It aligns with our need for caution, trust-building, and learning in our first year, providing a flexible foundation upon which to build more ambitious strategies as we gain experience.

3. LESSONS & REFLECTIONS:

This meeting has reinforced the critical role that company culture and psychological safety play in financial decision-making, especially in early-stage or uncertain contexts. I learned that decision-making dynamics benefit greatly from leaders who listen attentively and integrate diverse viewpoints, fostering trust and openness.

In future meetings, I will continue to emphasize the interplay between cultural and financial considerations, striving to frame my arguments in ways that highlight shared values and long-term benefits. I might also seek to engage earlier with the CEO and other shareholders to find common ground, potentially smoothing the path toward consensus.

A pattern I noticed is that shareholders value flexibility and adaptability highly when lacking historical data, while some prefer decisiveness to signal confidence. Recognizing this tension can help predict where divides might occur and allow me to tailor my approach accordingly.

Key takeaways for my future participation include the importance of balancing prudence with ambition, advocating for a culture of ongoing reflection, and supporting transparent communication. I will continue to champion approaches that safeguard resources while fostering collaboration and learning, ensuring our decisions build a resilient and confident team over time.

Shareholder Written Evaluations


Chad Cross's Evaluation

Chad Cross CEO STRENGTHS:
- Demonstrated strong facilitation skills by guiding thorough, multi-round discussions in each quarterly investment meeting, ensuring all shareholder perspectives were heard and thoughtfully synthesized (e.g., Q2 and Q4 investment meetings).
- Consistently maintained transparency and clarity in communication, particularly through detailed CEO summaries that outlined key arguments, consensus points, and next steps after each discussion round.
- Showed adaptability and responsiveness by shifting from an initial preference for more aggressive strategies (Option C, real estate) to supporting and implementing consensus decisions that reflected the team’s risk tolerance and cultural values (e.g., adopting Option A for the annual budget and supporting bonds in Q1 and Q3).

CEO AREAS FOR IMPROVEMENT:
- Could initiate earlier, more proactive dialogue around risk tolerance and the definition of monitoring safeguards before formal proposals, to streamline consensus-building and address shareholder concerns more efficiently (notably in Q2 and Q4 investment meetings).
- Would benefit from more directly surfacing and addressing potential dissent or operational readiness thresholds at the outset of discussions, to reduce the need for multiple rounds and accelerate alignment on final decisions.

COMPANY STRENGTHS:
- Fostered a disciplined, collaborative decision-making culture that prioritized psychological safety, trust, and inclusive debate, as evidenced by the iterative, consensus-driven process in each investment meeting.
- Maintained a prudent approach to risk management by favoring bonds in three out of four quarters, which aligned with company values of stability and learning and minimized exposure to unnecessary volatility.

COMPANY CONCERNS:
- Captured only 36.5% of the maximum possible returns for the year, indicating room for improvement in capital utilization and responsiveness to higher-return opportunities, especially when market conditions were favorable (e.g., stocks in Q1/Q3, real estate in Q2).
- The process for operationalizing transitions to higher-risk assets (e.g., real estate) was sometimes slow and required multiple rounds to reach consensus, which may have limited our ability to fully capitalize on market opportunities in real time.

QUESTIONS FOR CEO:
1. How do you plan to more proactively integrate early dialogue around risk appetite and operational safeguards to improve both decision speed and shareholder confidence next year?
2. What specific steps will you take to ensure we are better positioned to capture higher returns when market conditions clearly favor more ambitious asset allocations, without compromising our core values?

CEO Response to Evaluations


CEO Kelsie Schwartz responded to the evaluations and questions:

"Kelsie Schwartz Thank you all for your thoughtful, candid, and constructive evaluations. I deeply appreciate the time and care each of you dedicated to reflecting on both my leadership and our company’s performance this year. Your feedback is invaluable as we continue to evolve our approach and strive for excellence, not only in financial results but in the integrity and inclusivity of our decision-making culture.

**1. Feedback Themes:**

Across your evaluations, I see three recurring themes—both in what we did well and where we have room to grow:

- **Early Surfacing of Risk Tolerance and Safeguards:** Nearly every shareholder noted the need to identify and address differences in risk appetite, operational readiness, and monitoring expectations earlier in the process. This would help us accelerate consensus and improve decision efficiency, particularly before considering higher-risk investments.

- **Balancing Ambition with Disciplined Risk Management:** There is a shared recognition that our culture of prudent, collaborative decision-making fostered trust and psychological safety, but many of you expressed concern that this sometimes came at the expense of capturing higher returns, especially when market conditions favored more ambitious allocations.

- **Clarity and Inclusivity in Communication:** Many of you acknowledged the value of transparent, empathetic facilitation and clear synthesis of complex viewpoints. At the same time, several suggested that more proactively surfacing minority or dissenting perspectives—and clarifying decision criteria and escalation protocols up front—could further strengthen consensus and decision quality.

**2. Responses to Shareholder Questions:**

*Adrian McCann:*

1. **Improving Efficiency and Early Surfacing of Risk Tolerance/Safeguards:**
I plan to introduce a formal “risk and readiness alignment” segment at the very start of each investment cycle. This will invite all shareholders to explicitly articulate their current risk tolerance, operational concerns, and expectations for monitoring or escalation protocols before we discuss specific asset options. I will also provide a draft of proposed safeguards and decision criteria up front, so we can collaboratively refine these as a group and address any reservations early.

2. **Enhancing Ability to Capture Higher Returns While Maintaining Core Values:**
I will work to ensure that, once consensus around risk appetite and safeguards is established, we move more decisively to capture opportunities aligned with that consensus. This includes setting pre-agreed triggers for when we can shift to higher-return assets, and ensuring that all operational protocols are in place before implementation. Our goal will be to empower ambition when conditions allow, without sacrificing the trust and discipline that define our culture.

*Celine Nash:*

1. **Accelerating Alignment on Risk Appetite and Monitoring:**
I intend to facilitate structured pre-discussion surveys or roundtable check-ins on risk appetite and monitoring preferences before each meeting, so we can map areas of alignment and divergence in advance. This will help us identify where additional dialogue is needed and avoid iterative rounds by building common ground sooner.

2. **Ensuring Greater Investment Efficiency:**
I will propose a more dynamic review of market conditions at each meeting, highlighting where our existing safeguards and cultural readiness may support a more assertive allocation. By setting explicit criteria for when to pursue higher-return assets and reviewing these as a standing agenda item, we can act more quickly when the environment and our internal alignment support it.

*Clyde Vang:*

1. **Improving Early Surfacing of Risk Tolerance and Readiness:**
I will incorporate an “alignment checkpoint” at the outset of each cycle, where we openly discuss individual and collective comfort levels with risk, and identify any operational or cultural prerequisites for considering ambitious allocations. This will include directly inviting minority or dissenting perspectives to ensure authentic consensus.

2. **Ensuring Safeguards Are Operationalized and Communicated:**
I will provide written drafts of safeguards and monitoring protocols for higher-risk investments ahead of proposal rounds, and confirm their adoption through explicit shareholder approval. These will be published in a shared document repository to ensure clarity and accessibility for all.

*Chad Cross:*

1. **Proactively Integrating Early Dialogue on Risk Appetite and Safeguards:**
I plan to use structured facilitation tools—such as pre-meeting questionnaires and risk-mapping exercises—to surface risk preferences and operational concerns before formal proposals. This will allow us to address potential dissent and readiness thresholds at the outset, streamlining the path to consensus.

2. **Positioning for Higher Returns in Favorable Markets:**
I will recommend establishing pre-defined “readiness triggers” (e.g., unanimous confidence in safeguards, clear market signals) that, when met, allow us to pursue higher-return assets more swiftly. By formalizing these triggers and reviewing them regularly, we can be more agile in capitalizing on favorable conditions while upholding our core values.

*Ieuan Randolph:*

1. **Ensuring Earlier Alignment on Risk Tolerance and Safeguards:**
I will initiate each investment cycle with a dedicated session to clarify and document risk tolerance, operational safeguards, and monitoring expectations. These will be reviewed and agreed upon before any proposals for higher-risk assets are brought forward.

2. **Improving Capture of Market Returns While Maintaining Discipline:**
I will encourage the team to regularly benchmark our actual returns against market opportunities and use this analysis to inform our willingness to adjust allocations. By integrating this benchmarking into our decision process, we can balance ambition and discipline more effectively.

**3. Leadership Self-Assessment:**

- **Leadership Effectiveness:** I believe I provided calm, thoughtful guidance throughout the year, fostering a safe environment for open dialogue and ensuring that all voices were heard. My adaptability—shifting from Option C to A in the budget, or revising proposals to reflect shareholder concerns—demonstrates a willingness to lead through consensus rather than decree.

- **Decision Quality:** Our decisions reflected a careful balance of risk and return, aligning with our stated values and the available information at each point. While we did not always maximize returns, we avoided unnecessary volatility and preserved capital, which I believe was appropriate for our company’s first year.

- **Communication Clarity:** I consistently articulated the rationale behind proposals, summarized complex discussions, and provided transparent updates on performance and process. There is room to further clarify criteria and protocols earlier, but overall, I believe my communication supported understanding and alignment.

- **Responsiveness to Input:** I made a concerted effort to integrate shareholder perspectives, openly revising my own positions and proposals in response to feedback. This responsiveness was a cornerstone of our collaborative culture.

- **Adaptability:** I demonstrated flexibility in adjusting strategy based on both market conditions and evolving team consensus, as seen in our pivot from more aggressive to more cautious allocations and the adoption of co-created safeguards for higher-risk assets.

**4. Company Performance Assessment:**

- **Financial Results:** We achieved modest growth (1.25% increase in company value) and preserved capital, but captured only 36.5% of maximum possible returns. There is clear opportunity to improve our investment efficiency, particularly in stable markets.

- **Risk Management:** We maintained a disciplined approach, favoring stability and trust-building over aggressive risk-taking. This protected our capital but sometimes led to missed upside.

- **Strategic Alignment:** Our decisions were consistent with our strategic emphasis on trust, learning, and incremental progress, though we may have erred on the side of caution when conditions supported greater ambition.

- **Capital Utilization:** We utilized only 50% of available capital, reflecting our prioritization of flexibility and risk management. While this supported learning, it limited our return potential.

- **Market Responsiveness:** We responded thoughtfully to market signals, but our process for transitioning to higher-return assets was sometimes slow, resulting in missed opportunities.

**5. Future Direction and Improvements:**

Based on your feedback and my own reflections, I intend to implement the following improvements:

1. **Formalize Early Alignment on Risk and Readiness:**
I will introduce structured pre-meeting exercises and alignment checkpoints to surface and document risk tolerance, operational safeguards, and monitoring expectations before any asset-specific proposals. This will accelerate consensus and clarify expectations.

2. **Operationalize and Communicate Safeguards in Advance:**
For higher-risk investments, I will provide written drafts of safeguards and monitoring protocols ahead of proposal rounds, ensure explicit shareholder approval, and publish these for ongoing reference.

3. **Set Pre-Defined Triggers for Ambitious Allocations:**
We will establish clear readiness and market triggers that, when met, empower us to pursue higher-return assets decisively—provided all safeguards and shareholder confidence are in place. This will help us capture more market upside without compromising our core values.

In closing, I want to express my gratitude for your trust, candor, and partnership. Our collaborative, values-driven approach is our greatest strength, and I am committed to building on this foundation as we pursue both greater returns and deeper alignment in the year ahead. I look forward to continuing this journey with all of you, learning and growing together as a resilient and responsible team."

Meeting Conclusion


CEO Kelsie Schwartz concluded the meeting:

"Kelsie Schwartz Thank you all for your honest feedback and the care you brought to both your ratings and reflections. I’m grateful for the recognition of our strengths—especially in communication, risk management, and maintaining a culture of trust and inclusivity. At the same time, I take seriously the concerns about our financial results, capital utilization, and speed in capturing opportunities. In the coming year, I commit to surfacing risk tolerance and operational readiness much earlier, providing clear and proactive safeguards before we consider higher-risk moves, and setting explicit triggers to help us act more decisively when conditions align. I believe that by deepening our transparency, listening even more closely to every perspective, and embracing thoughtful ambition, we will achieve stronger results together. I look forward to building on our foundation and growing as a team—guided by empathy, integrity, and a shared commitment to learning and responsible progress."

The annual review meeting has concluded.
